Hi, doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me. Um, I hope I'm giving you the right information, but I guess... well, my sister actually encouraged me to come in.

Nothing dangerous, I promise, just... well, at first I was pacing around the apartment all night, every night, making all these notes for a supper club I wanted to start. Um... yeah, I guess you could say that. I should probably tell you—and I really hope this doesn't make me look bad, because I swear I'm usually a really good worker—but this all started right after I actually got fired from my waiting job.

I really hope I'm not making this confusing for you... I just wouldn't call it a bad crash or anything like that. Just a normal amount of tired from working on a project. Does that make sense? Like, I'm giving you the right kind of information, right?

It's actually a little embarrassing to admit, but my sister says it takes me forever just to get my shoes on before I head into work for my shift. \n\nI just want to get back to being a good employee and not worrying my family, you know? Is... is that the kind of thing you usually help people with? Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about this period where you were pacing around the apartment all night and making notes for a supper club?\n\nPatient: Yeah, of course. Um, I hope it doesn't sound too... intense or anything. It was actually a really creative time for me, I think. \n\nI just... I started texting a bunch of my old coworkers from the restaurant, trying to get them on board. And I was making all these spreadsheets, planning out tasting menus, even reaching out to possible musicians for entertainment. I was just really passionate about it, you know? \n\nBut I guess... well, I was trying to recruit people pretty much all day and night. It just felt like the whole supper club had to happen immediately, right that second. I mean, it's good to be driven and have goals, right? Does that sound like a normal thing to do?\n\nDoctor: Did you feel unusually happy or euphoric during the time you were planning the supper club?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess you could say that. I should probably tell you—and I really hope this doesn't make me look bad, because I swear I'm usually a really good worker—but this all started right after I actually got fired from my waiting job. \n\nI know I probably should have been upset about it, but honestly? I just felt weirdly unstoppable. Like, so excited about everything I was going to do. Um... no, not for two whole weeks. I really don't think so. 

I mean, everyone has a bad day here and there, right? Like if work is stressful or something, but... two weeks is a really long time to just be sad. I really pride myself on being able to bounce back. I don't like to wallow. I'd rather just put on a smile and get back to work, you know?

Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about this period where you were pacing around the apartment all night and making notes for a supper club?\n\nPatient: Yeah, of course. Um, I hope it doesn't sound too... intense or anything. It was actually a really creative time for me, I think. \n\nI just... I started texting a bunch of my old coworkers from the restaurant, trying to get them on board. And I was making all these spreadsheets, planning out tasting menus, even reaching out to possible musicians for entertainment. I was just really passionate about it, you know? \n\nBut I guess... well, I was trying to recruit people pretty much all day and night. It just felt like the whole supper club had to happen immediately, right that second. I mean, it's good to be driven and have goals, right? Does that sound like a normal thing to do?\n\nDoctor: Did you feel unusually happy or euphoric during the time you were planning the supper club?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess you could say that. I should probably tell you—and I really hope this doesn't make me look bad, because I swear I'm usually a really good worker—but this all started right after I actually got fired from my waiting job. \n\nI know I probably should have been upset about it, but honestly? I just felt weirdly unstoppable. Like, so excited about everything I was going to do. It was a really good feeling, mostly. \n\nBut... well, I feel a little awful admitting this part, but... if anyone questioned my plans or told me I needed to slow down, I... I'd kind of blow up at them. I'd react like they were trying to completely ruin my life. I'm not normally an angry person, I promise, I just... I really wanted the club to work out. I hope I'm not coming across as aggressive or anything. Honestly, the days kind of blurred together because I was just so focused on getting everything set up. I really hope that doesn't sound too disorganized of me. \n\nIt just felt like I was riding this really positive wave for a bit, you know? Until I started feeling so bogged down like I am now. But I wouldn't want to say it's some big, serious depression or anything. I'm usually pretty good at bouncing back. I just... But I wouldn't want to say it's some big, serious depression or anything. I'm usually pretty good at bouncing back. I just... I haven't really noticed any long periods of feeling really, really down in the past. Is it okay if I don't really have a good answer for that one? I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in your life where you felt sad or depressed for most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. Just a normal amount of tired from working on a project. Does that make sense?   },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, the... the heavy, slowed-down feeling? It's been, um... maybe about three months now? 

I know that sounds like a long time to let something go without seeing someone, and I probably should have come in sooner. I just kept thinking I could push through it, you know? Like maybe I just needed to drink more coffee before my shifts or get better sleep. I really didn't want to make a big deal out of nothing. 

But yeah, it's been about three months of feeling like I'm moving through molasses. I hope that's not... too bad? Like, it's okay that I waited, right? I just didn't want to be a bother until I was sure it wasn't going away on its own.

Does... does that timeframe sound about right to you? Like, is that a normal amount of time to be that focused on a new project?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods in your life where you felt consistently sad or depressed for an extended time?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I'm not really sure if I'd say \"consistently sad\" for an extended time. I mean, I hope I don't come across as someone who just mopes around, you know? I try really hard to stay positive.\n\nI guess... I mean, lately I've been feeling a bit bogged down, like I mentioned. Just a little low on energy, maybe? But I wouldn't want to say it's some big, serious depression or anything. I'm usually pretty good at bouncing back. I just... I haven't really noticed any long periods of feeling really, really down in the past. Is it okay if I don't really have a good answer for that one? I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in your life where you felt sad or depressed for most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, like I said, I've been feeling a little sluggish lately, but definitely not like... sad all day, every day. \n\nI really try to be a cheerful person, you know? I don't want to be someone who just brings everyone else down. Even when things are a little tough, I always try to look on the bright side. Just a normal amount of tired from working on a project. Does that make sense? No, I wouldn't say I felt sad or depressed before that, either. \n\nI mean, I really just try to take things as they come. I don't want to be the kind of person who dwells on things or gets stuck in a rut. I'm usually pretty upbeat! So, no, I didn't feel sad before this... sluggishness started. \n\nI just want to be sure I'm answering these questions right for you. It's okay that I haven't really had these long periods of being sad, isn't it? Um... no, not really. I mean, I wasn't feeling sad or anything before this. I was actually doing pretty good, like I said. 

I don't really get sad that often, honestly. I try to stay pretty positive. Even now, with this... heaviness, I wouldn't say I'm, like, crying all the time or feeling hopeless. I'm just tired, you know? Just really, really drained. 

I hope that makes sense? I just want to make sure I'm not making it sound worse than it is. I'm still showing up for my shifts at the restaurant and everything. Is it okay that I wasn't feeling depressed first? I hope I'm answering this right for you.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n18\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF31.0, F31.1, F31.2\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi, doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me. Um, I hope I'm giving you the right information, but I guess... well, my sister actually encouraged me to come in. \n\nIt's just that my body feels kind of out of my control lately? Nothing dangerous, I promise, just... well, at first I was pacing around the apartment all night, every night, making all these notes for a supper club I wanted to start. Which was actually kind of a productive phase, I think. But, um... lately, I'm just so slowed down. It's actually a little embarrassing to admit, but my sister says it takes me forever just to get my shoes on before I head into work for my shift. \n\nI just want to get back to being a good employee and not worrying my family, you know? Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about this period where you were pacing around the apartment all night and making notes for a supper club?\n\nPatient: Yeah, of course. Um, I hope it doesn't sound too... intense or anything. It was actually a really creative time for me, I think. \n\nI just... I started texting a bunch of my old coworkers from the restaurant, trying to get them on board. And I was making all these spreadsheets, planning out tasting menus, even reaching out to possible musicians for entertainment. I was just really passionate about it, you know? \n\nBut I guess... well, I was trying to recruit people pretty much all day and night. It just felt like the whole supper club had to happen immediately, right that second. I mean, it's good to be driven and have goals, right? Does that sound like a normal thing to do?\n\nDoctor: Did you feel unusually happy or euphoric during the time you were planning the supper club?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess you could say that. I should probably tell you—and I really hope this doesn't make me look bad, because I swear I'm usually a really good worker—but this all started right after I actually got fired from my waiting job. \n\nI know I probably should have been upset about it, but honestly? I just felt weirdly unstoppable. Like, so excited about everything I was going to do. It was a really good feeling, mostly. \n\nBut... well, I feel a little awful admitting this part, but... if anyone questioned my plans or told me I needed to slow down, I... I'd kind of blow up at them. I'd react like they were trying to completely ruin my life. I'm not normally an angry person, I promise, I just... I really wanted the club to work out. I hope I'm not coming across as aggressive or anything. Is... is it normal to react like that when you're just really passionate about a project?\n\nDoctor: How long did this unstoppable and excited feeling last after you got fired from your waiting job?\n\nPatient: Um, let's see... I'm trying to remember exactly. I want to make sure I'm giving you the right timeline. \n\nI think... well, it definitely went on for a little while. Maybe a couple of weeks? Or a few weeks? Honestly, the days kind of blurred together because I was just so focused on getting everything set up. I really hope that doesn't sound too disorganized of me. \n\nIt just felt like I was riding this really positive wave for a bit, you know? Until I started feeling so bogged down like I am now. Does... does that timeframe sound about right to you? Like, is that a normal amount of time to be that focused on a new project?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods in your life where you felt consistently sad or depressed for an extended time?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I'm not really sure if I'd say \"consistently sad\" for an extended time. I mean, I hope I don't come across as someone who just mopes around, you know? I try really hard to stay positive.\n\nI guess... I mean, lately I've been feeling a bit bogged down, like I mentioned. Just a little low on energy, maybe? But I wouldn't want to say it's some big, serious depression or anything. I'm usually pretty good at bouncing back. I just... I haven't really noticed any long periods of feeling really, really down in the past. Is it okay if I don't really have a good answer for that one? I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in your life where you felt sad or depressed for most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, like I said, I've been feeling a little sluggish lately, but definitely not like... sad all day, every day. \n\nI really try to be a cheerful person, you know? I don't want to be someone who just brings everyone else down. Even when things are a little tough, I always try to look on the bright side. So... no, I wouldn't say I've ever had a time like that where I was just depressed all day. I hope that's a good thing? Like, it's better that I haven't felt like that, right?\n\nDoctor: Did you experience any periods of feeling really down after the period where you felt unstoppable and excited about the supper club?\n\nPatient: Um, after the supper club stuff... well, not really down, no. I mean, I know I said earlier that I've been feeling a little bogged down lately, but I think that's mostly just because I tired myself out? \n\nLike, I was going at it so hard for those few weeks, so naturally I'd need a little break to catch my breath, right? But I definitely wouldn't say I felt really sad or depressed after. I always try to keep a good attitude and stay on the brighter side of things. \n\nI really hope I'm not making this confusing for you... I just wouldn't call it a bad crash or anything like that. Just a normal amount of tired from working on a project. Does that make sense? Like, I'm giving you the right kind of information, right?\n\nDoctor: Did you feel sad or depressed before you started feeling slowed down?\n\nPatient: Um... before I started feeling slowed down? No, I wouldn't say I felt sad or depressed before that, either. \n\nI mean, I really just try to take things as they come. I don't want to be the kind of person who dwells on things or gets stuck in a rut. I'm usually pretty upbeat! So, no, I didn't feel sad before this... sluggishness started. \n\nI just want to be sure I'm answering these questions right for you. It's okay that I haven't really had these long periods of being sad, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm being helpful.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt sad or depressed for at least two weeks at any point in your life?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not for two whole weeks. I really don't think so. \n\nI mean, everyone has a bad day here and there, right? Like if work is stressful or something, but... two weeks is a really long time to just be sad. I really pride myself on being able to bounce back. I don't like to wallow. I'd rather just put on a smile and get back to work, you know? \n\nSo, no, definitely not two weeks. I'm sorry if I'm sounding like a broken record with all these \"no's.\" I just want to be honest with you. Is it... is it normal that I'm saying no to all of this? I hope I'm not messing up your evaluation or anything.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel sad or depressed right before you started to feel slowed down?\n\nPatient: Um, right before I felt slowed down? I... I think we just talked about that, right? \n\nBut, um, no. No, I didn't feel sad right before. Like I was saying, I really try to keep a positive outlook. I don't want to be a downer or anything. I just... I kind of hit a wall with my energy, but my mood was fine. I promise I wasn't, like, miserable or anything. \n\nI hope it's okay that my answer is still the same. I'm really trying to give you exactly what you need to figure this out. I'm doing okay, right?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling slowed down?\n\nPatient: Um, the... the heavy, slowed-down feeling? It's been, um... maybe about three months now? \n\nI know that sounds like a long time to let something go without seeing someone, and I probably should have come in sooner. I just kept thinking I could push through it, you know? Like maybe I just needed to drink more coffee before my shifts or get better sleep. I really didn't want to make a big deal out of nothing. \n\nBut yeah, it's been about three months of feeling like I'm moving through molasses. I hope that's not... too bad? Like, it's okay that I waited, right? I just didn't want to be a bother until I was sure it wasn't going away on its own.\n\nDoctor: Has your slowed-down feeling lasted for most of every day?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess maybe a good chunk of the day? But not, like, every single second. \n\nI mean, I still manage to get through my shifts at the restaurant, you know? I put on a smile for the customers, and I try not to let it show. It's just... underneath that, I feel really sluggish. But I'm still functioning! I don't want you to think I'm just lying around all day doing nothing. \n\nSo, I guess it's there most of the day, but I'm really trying my best to fight it. Is that... does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining it right.\n\nDoctor: Did your slowed-down feeling start after your period of feeling unstoppable and excited about the supper club ended?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not right after. I mean, the supper club thing... that was a while ago. \n\nI kind of had this period where I was just... normal, I guess? Like, I wasn't super hyped up anymore, but I wasn't feeling like this yet either. I was just going to work, doing my thing. I thought I had everything balanced out, to be honest. I was really proud of myself for getting back into a good routine.\n\nSo, yeah, there was a gap between the two. I didn't just crash straight from one into the other. I hope that's a good sign? Like, it means I was doing okay for a bit, right?\n\nDoctor: Did your slowed-down feeling start after you stopped being excited about the supper club or was it triggered by something else?\n\nPatient: Um... I've been trying to figure that out myself, to be honest. I don't think there was any big, dramatic trigger or anything. Nothing bad happened at work or, you know, in my personal life. \n\nEverything was actually going pretty well, which is why I feel a little bad even complaining about feeling this way. It just sort of... crept up on me, I guess? One week I was fine, and then gradually I just started feeling heavier. \n\nI really wish I could give you a better reason for why it started. Is it... is it okay if it just happens without a real reason? I hope I'm still giving you the right information, I just really can't pinpoint a specific thing that set it off.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel like you were grieving or reacting to something when your slowed-down feeling started?\n\nPatient: Um, no, definitely not grieving. Everyone in my family is doing fine, thankfully. And... yeah, I wasn't really reacting to anything specific. I know other people have it way worse, and I'm really trying to just push through it at work. \n\nI hope it's not frustrating for you that I can't give you a clear reason. Is it... is it normal for this to just happen without, like, a tragedy or something setting it off? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel sad or depressed at any point before you started feeling slowed down?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, I wasn't feeling sad or anything before this. I was actually doing pretty good, like I said. \n\nI don't really get sad that often, honestly. I try to stay pretty positive. Even now, with this... heaviness, I wouldn't say I'm, like, crying all the time or feeling hopeless. I'm just tired, you know? Just really, really drained. \n\nI hope that makes sense? I just want to make sure I'm not making it sound worse than it is. I'm still showing up for my shifts at the restaurant and everything. Is it okay that I wasn't feeling depressed first? I hope I'm answering this right for you.\n\nDoctor: Has your slowed-down feeling been present every day for the past three months?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, pretty much every day. I'd say the heavy, slowed-down part has been there for about three months now. \n\nI mean, I still get up and go to work, I promise I'm not just laying in bed all day or anything like that. I'm still trying my best to be productive. It's just... it's just been a constant sort of drag for the last three months. \n\nIs three months a long time for this kind of thing? I hope I haven't let it go on too long before coming in to talk to you. I just kept thinking I could snap myself out of it, you know? I hope that's okay.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your slowed-down feeling could be related to feeling depressed or is it something else entirely?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't think it's depression, really. Like I said, I'm not really sad or crying or anything. I think it's just... I don't know, maybe I'm just burnt out from work? Being on your feet all day at the restaurant can really take it out of you, you know? \n\nI just feel like it's something physical, like I just need to catch up on sleep or take some vitamins or something. I'm really trying not to make a big deal out of it. Do you think it could just be exhaustion? I don't want to waste your time if it's just me being lazy or something. I hope I'm explaining this okay.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I started texting a bunch of my old coworkers from the restaurant, trying to get them on board. And I was making all these spreadsheets, planning out tasting menus, even reaching out to possible musicians for entertainment. I was just really passionate about it, you know? \n\nBut I guess... well, I was trying to recruit people pretty much all day and night. It just felt like the whole supper club had to happen immediately, right that second. I mean, it's good to be driven and have goals, right? Does that sound like a normal thing to do?\n\nDoctor: Did you feel unusually happy or euphoric during the time you were planning the supper club?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess you could say that. I should probably tell you—and I really hope this doesn't make me look bad, because I swear I'm usually a really good worker—but this all started right after I actually got fired from my waiting job. \n\nI know I probably should have been upset about it, but honestly? I just felt weirdly unstoppable. Like, so excited about everything I was going to do. It was a really good feeling, mostly. \n\nBut... well, I feel a little awful admitting this part, but... if anyone questioned my plans or told me I needed to slow down, I... I'd kind of blow up at them. I'd react like they were trying to completely ruin my life. I'm not normally an angry person, I promise, I just... I really wanted the club to work out. I hope I'm not coming across as aggressive or anything. Is... is it normal to react like that when you're just really passionate about a project?\n\nDoctor: How long did this unstoppable and excited feeling last after you got fired from your waiting job?\n\nPatient: Um, let's see... I'm trying to remember exactly. I want to make sure I'm giving you the right timeline. \n\nI think... well, it definitely went on for a little while. Maybe a couple of weeks? Or a few weeks? Honestly, the days kind of blurred together because I was just so focused on getting everything set up. I really hope that doesn't sound too disorganized of me. \n\nIt just felt like I was riding this really positive wave for a bit, you know? Until I started feeling so bogged down like I am now. Does... does that timeframe sound about right to you? Like, is that a normal amount of time to be that focused on a new project?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods in your life where you felt consistently sad or depressed for an extended time?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I'm not really sure if I'd say \"consistently sad\" for an extended time. I mean, I hope I don't come across as someone who just mopes around, you know? I try really hard to stay positive.\n\nI guess... I mean, lately I've been feeling a bit bogged down, like I mentioned. Just a little low on energy, maybe? But I wouldn't want to say it's some big, serious depression or anything. I'm usually pretty good at bouncing back. I just... I haven't really noticed any long periods of feeling really, really down in the past. Is it okay if I don't really have a good answer for that one? I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in your life where you felt sad or depressed for most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, like I said, I've been feeling a little sluggish lately, but definitely not like... sad all day, every day. \n\nI really try to be a cheerful person, you know? I don't want to be someone who just brings everyone else down. Even when things are a little tough, I always try to look on the bright side. So... no, I wouldn't say I've ever had a time like that where I was just depressed all day. I hope that's a good thing? Like, it's better that I haven't felt like that, right?\n\nDoctor: Did you experience any periods of feeling really down after the period where you felt unstoppable and excited about the supper club?\n\nPatient: Um, after the supper club stuff... well, not really down, no. I mean, I know I said earlier that I've been feeling a little bogged down lately, but I think that's mostly just because I tired myself out? \n\nLike, I was going at it so hard for those few weeks, so naturally I'd need a little break to catch my breath, right? But I definitely wouldn't say I felt really sad or depressed after. I always try to keep a good attitude and stay on the brighter side of things. \n\nI really hope I'm not making this confusing for you... I just wouldn't call it a bad crash or anything like that. Just a normal amount of tired from working on a project. Does that make sense? Like, I'm giving you the right kind of information, right?\n\nDoctor: Did you feel sad or depressed before you started feeling slowed down?\n\nPatient: Um... before I started feeling slowed down? No, I wouldn't say I felt sad or depressed before that, either. \n\nI mean, I really just try to take things as they come. I don't want to be the kind of person who dwells on things or gets stuck in a rut. I'm usually pretty upbeat! So, no, I didn't feel sad before this... sluggishness started. \n\nI just want to be sure I'm answering these questions right for you. It's okay that I haven't really had these long periods of being sad, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm being helpful.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt sad or depressed for at least two weeks at any point in your life?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not for two whole weeks. I really don't think so. \n\nI mean, everyone has a bad day here and there, right? Like if work is stressful or something, but... two weeks is a really long time to just be sad. I really pride myself on being able to bounce back. I don't like to wallow. I'd rather just put on a smile and get back to work, you know? \n\nSo, no, definitely not two weeks. I'm sorry if I'm sounding like a broken record with all these \"no's.\" I just want to be honest with you. Is it... is it normal that I'm saying no to all of this? I hope I'm not messing up your evaluation or anything.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel sad or depressed right before you started to feel slowed down?\n\nPatient: Um, right before I felt slowed down? I... I'm really trying to give you exactly what you need to figure this out. I'm doing okay, right?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling slowed down?\n\nPatient: Um, the... the heavy, slowed-down feeling? It's been, um... maybe about three months now? \n\nI know that sounds like a long time to let something go without seeing someone, and I probably should have come in sooner. I just kept thinking I could push through it, you know? Like maybe I just needed to drink more coffee before my shifts or get better sleep. I really didn't want to make a big deal out of nothing. \n\nBut yeah, it's been about three months of feeling like I'm moving through molasses. I hope that's not... too bad? Like, it's okay that I waited, right? I guess maybe a good chunk of the day? But not, like, every single second. \n\nI mean, I still manage to get through my shifts at the restaurant, you know? I put on a smile for the customers, and I try not to let it show. It's just... underneath that, I feel really sluggish. But I'm still functioning! I don't want you to think I'm just lying around all day doing nothing. \n\nSo, I guess it's there most of the day, but I'm really trying my best to fight it. Is that... does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining it right.\n\nDoctor: Did your slowed-down feeling start after your period of feeling unstoppable and excited about the supper club ended?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not right after. I mean, the supper club thing... that was a while ago. \n\nI kind of had this period where I was just... normal, I guess? Like, I wasn't super hyped up anymore, but I wasn't feeling like this yet either. I was just going to work, doing my thing. I thought I had everything balanced out, to be honest. I was really proud of myself for getting back into a good routine.\n\nSo, yeah, there was a gap between the two. I didn't just crash straight from one into the other. I hope that's a good sign? Like, it means I was doing okay for a bit, right?\n\nDoctor: Did your slowed-down feeling start after you stopped being excited about the supper club or was it triggered by something else?\n\nPatient: Um... I've been trying to figure that out myself, to be honest. I don't think there was any big, dramatic trigger or anything. Nothing bad happened at work or, you know, in my personal life. \n\nEverything was actually going pretty well, which is why I feel a little bad even complaining about feeling this way. Is it... is it okay if it just happens without a real reason? I hope I'm still giving you the right information, I just really can't pinpoint a specific thing that set it off.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel like you were grieving or reacting to something when your slowed-down feeling started?\n\nPatient: Um, no, definitely not grieving. Everyone in my family is doing fine, thankfully. And... yeah, I wasn't really reacting to anything specific. No breakups or fights or anything like that. \n\nThat's kind of why I feel a little guilty even talking about it, you know? Is it... is it normal for this to just happen without, like, a tragedy or something setting it off? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers.\n\nDoctor: Did you feel sad or depressed at any point before you started feeling slowed down?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, I wasn't feeling sad or anything before this. I was actually doing pretty good, like I said. \n\nI don't really get sad that often, honestly. I try to stay pretty positive. Even now, with this... heaviness, I wouldn't say I'm, like, crying all the time or feeling hopeless. I'm just tired, you know? Just really, really drained. \n\nI hope that makes sense? I just want to make sure I'm not making it sound worse than it is. I'm still showing up for my shifts at the restaurant and everything. Is it okay that I wasn't feeling depressed first? I hope I'm answering this right for you.\n\nDoctor: Has your slowed-down feeling been present every day for the past three months?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, pretty much every day. I hope that's okay.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your slowed-down feeling could be related to feeling depressed or is it something else entirely?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't think it's depression, really. I hope I'm explaining this okay.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your slowed-down feeling could be related to feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: Um, I'm sorry, I think I just answered that, but maybe I didn't explain it right. I really don't think it's depression. I'm not feeling down or hopeless or anything like that. \n\nI just... I think it's just physical tiredness, you know? Like I've just been working too hard. I'm trying to be as honest as I can so you can help me figure this out.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your slowed-down feeling could be related to a period of depression that you might not have recognized as such?\n\nPatient: Um...
